# Voice Step 2 Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build voice delivery step 2 (spec §11 item 2): a spike on the real signed bundle, then a Swift bridge for capture and on-device transcription, the hold-key event tap and the toggle shortcut, the §6.2 recording rules, the §6.4 permissions, and a HUD Listening state, so that a spoken phrase reaches the step-1 fast path.

**Architecture:** The rules stay in plain Rust that Linux CI tests. `acd-voice` gains `listen.rs`: the trigger events, the hold-key state, the recorder state machine (60 s cap, short and silent utterances, generation filtering, final timeout) and the `SpeechInput` trait with a fake. The executor gains a press-time `listen` and a `resume`. `acd-voice-client`'s controller freezes the snapshot at the press, drives a `SpeechInput`, and feeds the final transcript into the same utterance path that typed text uses. In the app, a Swift package (`app/src-tauri/swift`) is compiled into a static library with a hand-written C ABI. Swift owns AVAudioEngine, SpeechAnalyzer and SpeechTranscriber, the event tap and the lock and sleep observers. Audio never leaves Swift. Rust gets typed events through one `'static` callback into a bounded queue.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2024 (workspace `rust-version = "1.90"`), tokio; Swift 6.4 toolchain with Swift 5 language mode, SwiftPM, XCTest; AVFoundation, Speech (SpeechAnalyzer, SpeechTranscriber, AssetInventory; macOS 26), CoreGraphics event taps, IOKit HID access checks; `swift-rs = "=1.0.8"` as a build dependency only; Tauri 2.12.1, tauri-nspanel 2.1.1, tauri-plugin-global-shortcut 2.4.0; Svelte 5, vitest 5.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-voice-design.md` (authoritative), mainly §3, §4.1, §4.2, §4.4, §6.2, §6.3, §6.4, §8, §9, §10. Read the step-1 plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-voice-step1.md` for D1–D25, which still hold. This plan's decisions are numbered S1, S2, … so they never collide with step 1's.

## Global Constraints

Quoted from the spec. Every task's requirements include this section.

- "macOS 26 or later, on a Mac that supports SpeechTranscriber." (§2.1)
- "Nothing is heard unless the user holds or toggles the trigger." (§3.5)
- "Audio never leaves the machine. Transcription is on-device only; there is no fallback to a server recognizer." (§3.6)
- "Transcripts, plans and spoken text are never written to logs, files or settings, and never appear in tracing, `Debug` output, error strings or the frontend console. Logs carry tags, lengths, timings and outcomes only." (§3.7) In Swift this means no `print`, `NSLog`, `os_log` or `Logger` call with transcript text, and no `localizedDescription` of a Speech or AVFoundation error anywhere.
- "Every step passes the same client checks as a key press." (§3.2) Speech feeds the step-1 path unchanged.
- "Swift owns every `Task`, `AsyncSequence` and framework object. Rust holds opaque session handles and receives typed callbacks." (§4.2)
- "Every callback carries a session generation id; results from an older generation are discarded." (§4.2)
- "Queues between Swift and Rust are bounded. The audio callback never blocks on Rust or model work." (§4.2)
- "AppKit work runs on the main thread, as `panel.rs` does today." (§4.2)
- "Buffer ownership, callback lifetime and cancellation are part of the bridge contract and its tests." (§4.2)
- "Backends that cannot stream partials still work; the HUD then shows "listening" without live text." (§4.1)
- "It never activates the app. While listening, thinking, speaking or showing an error, it does not take keyboard focus either." (§4.4)
- "Pressing the voice trigger again while a voice countdown runs cancels that countdown at once, before any new recognition." (§4.4)
- "Recording happens only while the hold key is held, or between the two presses of the toggle shortcut. Releasing ends it." (§6.2)
- "A recording longer than 60 s stops on its own (a lost key-up must not leave the microphone on)." (§6.2)
- "A live microphone indicator shows in the HUD and the menu bar icon." (§6.2)
- "Utterances under 0.3 s or silent are ignored." (§6.2)
- "Locking the screen or sleep stops capture and speech." (§6.2)
- "Provider error bodies, Swift errors and model output are never logged or shown verbatim; the HUD shows a classified error." (§6.3)
- "Each is requested when voice is first turned on, with an explanation in Settings. The toggle shortcut works without Input Monitoring." (§6.4)
- "Voice development and delivery use a stable signing identity (an Apple Development certificate)." (§6.4) Install with `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY="Apple Development: Toni Bergholm (RLYXB8JS76)" just app-install`.
- Step-1 AppKit lesson: `show_and_make_key` makes the panel's content view first responder. Any new path that makes a panel key must call `crate::panel::focus_webview`. This plan adds no new key path: Listening never takes key (§4.4), and the HUD keeps using `voice::hud::apply`.
- Repository rules (`CLAUDE.md`): do not run `cargo fmt` on the workspace, and keep diffs to the lines you need. Every behaviour change has a test, and a test for a fix fails without the fix.
- Ready means `cargo test --workspace --locked` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` pass. For changes under `app/` (including `app/src-tauri/swift`), `cd app && npm ci && npm run verify` must also pass. That is macOS only; on Linux, report it as pending for CI.

## Review Focus

1. **The key-up is lost, or the release is processed late** (the tap missed it, the app was busy, or the key stuck). The microphone stops at 60 s, the recording is thrown away, and nothing is routed. That holds whichever runs first: the timer, the release or the final. Pinned in Task 2 (`a_recording_past_60_s_is_cut_and_discarded`, `a_release_after_the_cap_is_too_long_even_before_the_timer`, `a_final_after_the_cap_is_too_long`) and Task 4 (`the_cap_stops_the_microphone_and_sends_nothing`).
2. **The user types with the hold key held** (Right Option + e types "é"), **or that key event is lost in a full queue.** The recording is cancelled, nothing is routed, and the later key-up neither stops nor routes anything. Pinned in Task 2 (`a_chord_cancels_and_the_release_does_nothing`), Task 4 (`typing_with_the_hold_key_down_cancels_the_recording`, `an_overflow_cancels_the_recording_and_its_final_never_routes`), Task 5B (`testOverflowIsLatchedAndDeliveredFirst`) and Task 6 (`an_overflow_is_reported_before_the_next_event`).
3. **The trigger is pressed during a voice countdown, then the user says "cancel".** The countdown is cancelled at the press, before any audio is transcribed. The HUD then says "Cancelled", not "Nothing to cancel". A press that is too short says "Cancelled" too. Pinned in Task 3 (`the_trigger_cancels_a_countdown_at_press_and_a_spoken_cancel_reports_it`, `a_short_press_after_cancelling_a_countdown_says_cancelled`) and Task 4 (`the_trigger_cancels_a_running_countdown_before_anything_is_heard`).
4. **A late result from an older recording, or a final that reaches admission after a newer press, a lock or voice off and on.** It is dropped and never routed, and it never answers a newer confirmation. Pinned in Task 2 (`events_from_an_older_session_are_dropped`), Task 4 (`a_late_final_from_a_cancelled_recording_routes_nothing`, `a_final_admitted_after_a_newer_press_is_dropped`, `a_final_admitted_after_a_lock_is_dropped`, `a_final_admitted_after_voice_off_and_on_is_dropped`) and Task 5B (`testSessionEventsOfAnotherGenerationAreDropped`).
5. **The screen locks, the Mac sleeps, or voice is turned off mid-recording or mid-step.** Capture stops in Swift without waiting for Rust. The menu bar indicator clears when Swift reports capture stopped. Nothing is routed. Voice countdowns that have not fired are cancelled, a thinking step is dropped, and transcripts, snapshots and shown confirmations are forgotten. Pinned in Task 4 (`screen_lock_clears_a_waiting_confirm`, `screen_lock_cancels_an_unsent_countdown`, `screen_lock_drops_a_thinking_step`, `screen_lock_cancels_the_recording`, `voice_off_mid_recording_stops_the_microphone`) and Task 5C (`testCancelAllReachesFinishingSessions`).

## Decisions made in this plan

Task text refers to these by number. Step-1 decisions D1–D25 still hold. Decisions use the prefix S so they never collide with step 1's D numbers.

- **S1. The bridge is a C ABI static library. `swift-rs` only builds and links it.**
  - `app/src-tauri/swift` is a SwiftPM package with a `.static` library product `VoiceBridge`. Its Swift functions are exported with `@_cdecl`. A header-only C target, `VoiceBridgeC`, holds the contract (`voice_bridge.h`), so Swift compiles against the same struct and constants that Rust mirrors.
  - `build.rs` calls `swift_rs::SwiftLinker::new("13.0").with_package("VoiceBridge", "swift").link()`. swift-rs 1.0.8 is already in `app/src-tauri/Cargo.lock` through Tauri, so the build adds no new crate version. It handles the Swift runtime search paths and two Xcode 27 quirks: SwiftPM's changed product layout, and `@_cdecl` symbols that become local in static products (`globalize_cdecl_symbols`).
  - swift-rs's runtime types (`SRString`, `SRObject`) are not used. §4.2 needs callbacks from tasks that Swift owns, tagged with a generation. A function pointer and a plain struct do that with no reference counts crossing the boundary.
- **S2. Audio stays in Swift, and Rust sees one `SpeechInput` seam** (see "Spec issues found", item 1).
  - SpeechAnalyzer consumes AVAudioEngine buffers in the same process. Sending frames through Rust would add a copy per buffer and a path where the audio thread waits on Rust.
  - Inside Swift, capture (`AudioCapture`, AVAudioEngine) and transcription (`SpeechWork`, SpeechAnalyzer) are separate protocols. XCTest drives the real `Transcription` from a file, and drives the session lifecycle with fakes of both.
  - Backend B can implement `SpeechInput` by combining `cpal` and `transcribe-rs` behind it.
- **S3. The session handle is a `u64` generation, not a pointer. There is one callback sink for the life of the process.**
  - Rust passes a non-zero generation to `vb_session_start`, `vb_session_stop` and `vb_session_cancel`. Stop and cancel for an unknown generation do nothing.
  - `vb_init` is called once, on the main thread, with a `'static` Rust function and a null context. There is no context to free, so no callback can outlive its target.
  - Text in an event is a borrowed UTF-8 pointer, valid only during the callback. Rust copies it before returning.
- **S4. Safety commands never wait in a queue.** (Revised after the Sol review.)
  - Swift keeps every session that holds the microphone or still has work in `Sessions`, a registry behind an `NSLock`. Stop, cancel and stop-all (lock, sleep) take that lock, find the session and stop its capture synchronously, before the C call returns. No queue sits between the call and the microphone, so nothing can drop them.
  - Stop ends capture and leaves the session in the registry while its transcript finishes. Cancel, the final timeout, a lock and a replacing start all reach a finishing session: they stop capture if it still runs, cancel the session's task and its analyzer, and remove it. "Capture ended" (`stopCapture`) and "all work cancelled" (`cancel`) are separate operations.
  - `vb_session_start` cancels every other session first. Each old capture is stopped synchronously, inside the start call, before the new session's task can open the microphone.
  - Capabilities and asset downloads run on their own tasks (`Assets`), never on the session path, so a slow download cannot delay a stop.
- **S5. Every queue between Swift and Rust is bounded, and an overflow fails closed.** (Revised after the Sol review.)
  - Swift's outbox has a fixed shape: one pending capture state (latest wins), one pending partial (latest wins), one latched lock or sleep flag, and a FIFO of at most 64 other events. At most one drain block is queued on the serial event queue at any time. The audio thread never touches it: partials come from the transcription task.
  - When the FIFO is full, Swift drops the new event and latches an overflow flag. The next drain delivers `VB_EV_OVERFLOW` first.
  - The Rust callback calls `try_send` on a tokio `mpsc::channel(64)` and never blocks. A failed send latches `Link::overflow` and wakes the pump, which hands `BridgeEvent::Overflow` to the controller before any later event.
  - `Controller::overflow` treats a lost event as the worst case: it might have been a chord's other key, a key-up, a lock or a final. It cancels the active recording, resets the hold-key state and bumps the admission epoch (S23). A transcript already on its way to routing is dropped too.
- **S6. The snapshot freezes at the press.** Spec §4.3 says voice freezes the snapshot "when recording starts". `Controller::hold` and `Controller::toggle` read `panel_open` and freeze at the press. The transcript later routes against that snapshot. Typed text keeps freezing at submit (it has no press).
- **S7. What a press does to the step on screen** (spec §4.4, extended):
  - A voice countdown, or a countdown step not yet acknowledged (D22 `Presenting`), is cancelled at once. The next utterance's "cancel" then reports `Cancelled`, because the flag that `begin` reads survives until that utterance.
  - A step still thinking is dropped. Its route result arrives for an old utterance number and is ignored.
  - A step that waits for input (confirm, which-one) stays on screen under the Listening state (`Hud::Listening { over }`), read-only, so the user can see what "confirm" or "the second one" refers to. Its buttons are disabled while listening.
  - A step already fired keeps waiting for its outcome (step-1 final review B4).
- **S8. Ignored recordings restore the screen; failed recordings fail closed.**
  - Too short, silent, empty, or cancelled (chord, HUD Cancel, Esc, overflow): `Executor::resume` shows the step that waits again, or "Cancelled" if the press cancelled a countdown, or hides the HUD.
  - Microphone or speech errors, the 60 s cap and the final timeout: `Executor::listen_failed` shows the error and ends any step that waits. Nothing can then be confirmed off screen.
- **S9. The 60 s cap is enforced at every point, and a capped recording is discarded.** (Revised after the Sol review.)
  - Rust checks the cap in three places: the timer (`Recorder::tick`), the release (`Recorder::stop` returns `Stopped::TooLong` at or after 60 s), and every event (`Recorder::event` returns `TooLong` when the recording has run 60 s, or when a final reports 60 s of audio or more). The order the timer and events run in no longer matters.
  - A capped recording is never routed. A stuck key and a 60 s monologue look the same, and a fast-path phrase in a long accidental recording would reach a countdown.
  - Swift's failsafe starts when capture starts, before the analyzer starts, and fires at `max_ms` (60 s). It stops capture and cancels the session, so a stuck Rust cannot leave the microphone on.
- **S10. "Silent" means less than 150 ms of voiced audio** (`MIN_VOICED`). A buffer counts as voiced when its RMS is at least -45 dBFS. Swift measures `audio_ms` and `voiced_ms`, and Rust applies the rules (`judge`). The thresholds are constants, and the live check in Task 10 records whether they need tuning.
- **S11. A chord cancels, and the tap never forwards key codes.** The listen-only tap watches `flagsChanged` and `keyDown`. For the hold key it forwards down or up. While the hold key is down it forwards "another key" for any other key, with no key code. While it is up it forwards nothing. Another key while the hold key is down cancels the recording, and the following key-up does nothing.
- **S12. Hold key and toggle shortcut settings.**
  - The hold key is one of Right Option (default), Left Option, Right Command, Right Control, Fn, or Off.
  - The toggle shortcut defaults to `Control+Alt+V` (pending user approval, see "Deviations pending user approval"). It is refused if it equals the inbox hotkey, and the inbox hotkey is refused if it equals the toggle shortcut.
  - Both are active only while voice is on. The hold key also needs Input Monitoring; the toggle shortcut does not (spec §6.4).
- **S13. Deferred from spec §8 to later work, and said so in Settings:** Space as a hold key while the inbox panel is open; choosing a microphone device (the system default is used); choosing the speech language (English only: the system locale if it is an English locale SpeechTranscriber supports, else en-US); a level meter in the HUD; voice, rate and alert settings (step 4); the model setting (step 3). Pending user approval.
- **S14. Speech Recognition permission is conditional on the spike.** By default the plan assumes the SpeechTranscriber path does not need it, because SpeechAnalyzer runs on-device. `voice::permissions::SPEECH_PERMISSION_REQUIRED` holds the spike's answer.
  - If the spike shows transcription fails, or prompts, while Speech Recognition is not granted, set it to `true`. Settings then shows the Speech Recognition row, turning voice on asks for it, and `vb_session_start` refuses with `VB_ERR_SPEECH_DENIED` until it is granted.
  - `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription` is in `Info.plist` either way. Calling `SFSpeechRecognizer.requestAuthorization` without it ends the process.
- **S15. The utterance source is a log tag only.** `Source::{Typed, Speech}` appears in the `utterance routed` log line. `safety::Origin` (`Fast`, `Model`) is unchanged, and so is every confirmation level. Task 4 checks that a spoken "approve" produces the same proposal as a typed one, and that the D22 acknowledgement and the live revalidation still guard speech.
- **S16. The build works without the macOS 26 SDK, and the app's minimum macOS becomes 13.0** (pending user approval).
  - SpeechAnalyzer code sits under `#if compiler(>=6.2)`, which ships with the macOS 26 SDK, and under `if #available(macOS 26.0, *)`. Older SDKs build a stub that reports `sdk_too_old`. If CI's `macos-latest` image still has an older Xcode, the app still builds there.
  - The Swift package targets macOS 13, and `tauri.conf.json` sets `bundle.macOS.minimumSystemVersion` to `"13.0"`, so the static library and the app agree on the deployment target. Tauri's default was 10.13.
  - FoundationModels (probe only, Task 1A) is weak-linked: autolinking is turned off for it, and the app links it with `-weak_framework`, so the app still launches where the framework does not exist.
  - Swift 5 language mode keeps C callbacks and AVFoundation from raising Swift 6 `Sendable` errors. That mode proves nothing about races, so every piece of shared state names its guard: an `NSLock`, an actor, the main actor, or one owning thread.
- **S17. Input Monitoring.** Swift reads its status with `IOHIDCheckAccess(kIOHIDRequestTypeListenEvent)`, the only call that separates "not asked" from "denied". It asks with `CGRequestListenEventAccess()`. Settings refreshes the status when its window gains focus, and the app retries the tap when the status turns to granted. If the spike shows the tap needs a relaunch after the grant, set `voice::permissions::TAP_NEEDS_RELAUNCH` to `true`. Settings then says "Quit and reopen Agent Caller to use the hold key" instead of retrying.
- **S18. The microphone indicators show what Swift reports.** (Revised after the Sol review.)
  - Swift emits `VB_EV_CAPTURE` (value 1 or 0) when AVAudioEngine actually starts or stops for a session. The outbox keeps the latest capture state in its own slot, so it is never dropped.
  - `Controller::capture` drives `HudSink::listening`, and with it the menu bar dot. Rust never turns the indicator on or off from its own commands. A capture-on report for a recording Rust no longer has is answered with a cancel.
  - The menu bar icon gets a red dot in its top-right corner. `tray::render` draws the summary and the dot together, so a view update never erases the dot. The HUD shows its microphone mark in the Listening state. macOS also shows its own orange indicator.
- **S19. Permission errors in the HUD can open System Settings.** The only panes that can be opened are three fixed `x-apple.systempreferences:` URLs (Microphone, Speech Recognition, Input Monitoring). Commands take a `PermissionKind` enum, never a URL.
- **S20. The probe is gated and serialized.** Task 1A adds the probe to Settings, shown only while voice is on and "Show the typed command field" is on. `voice_probe` checks both settings too. Swift runs one probe at a time and refuses the microphone steps while a voice session exists. The clip probe deletes its audio file. Reports carry statuses, counts, timings and a `matched` flag, never a transcript. The probe stays after the spike as a diagnostic.
- **S21. Assets.** If the English speech model is not installed, voice is unavailable with `assets_missing`, and Settings offers "Download speech model". That calls `AssetInventory.assetInstallationRequest(supporting:)` on the `Assets` task, never on the session path. There is no "English until installed" fallback, because English is the only language (see "Spec issues found"). Pending user approval.
- **S22. Spike outcomes that change or stop the plan.** S14 and S17 cover the two permission questions. Three more:
  - **Grants reset after a rebuild with the same identity**, with a changed CDHash and an unchanged designated requirement. Stop before Task 5A and escalate to the orchestrator. Every later task assumes stable grants. The first suspects are the designated requirement and the bundle identifier.
  - **Live transcription fails in the bundle with every permission granted**, or the clip run fails too. Stop before Task 5A and escalate. Record the probe's error tags and the locales.
  - **Partials do not stream** (`partials: 0` while `matched: true`). Set `Assets.partialsStream` (Task 5B) to `false`, so Settings does not promise live text. Nothing else changes: the HUD already shows "Listening…" without text (spec §4.1). The default is `true`.
  - Foundation Models availability is probed in the bundle (spec §11 item 2) and recorded. Its answer changes nothing in step 2; step 3 uses it.
- **S23. A final carries its admission token.** (New after the Sol review.) When the recorder yields a transcript, the controller records `Admission { session, epoch }` under the core lock and passes it to `utterance`. Admission compares it with the current values under the same lock and drops the transcript if either changed. The epoch grows with every new recording, every lock or sleep, every overflow and every voice on or off. An old "confirm" can therefore never answer a newer confirmation.
- **S24. Lock and sleep invalidate voice.** (New after the Sol review.) `Controller::system_stop` does what turning voice off does, but leaves voice on:
  - it cancels the recording and resets the hold-key state;
  - it bumps the session and the epoch, so an in-flight route is dropped and its model call cancelled (step-1 B4);
  - `Executor::reset` cancels a voice countdown that has not fired, and forgets the executor's transcript, snapshot and any confirmation or which-one list on screen;
  - the HUD hides.
  A voice step that already fired keeps running in acd-client, since it cannot be undone. Its outcome is no longer shown. Swift has already stopped capture on its own (S4) by the time Rust hears of the lock.
- **S25. Esc cancels a recording.** (New after the Sol review.) Listening never takes key (spec §4.4; step 1 learned that taking key for a passing state bounced focus and closed the inbox). Three paths cancel a recording:
  - Esc in the inbox panel. The `escape` command first calls `Controller::escape_listening`, which cancels through the recorder, then falls back to the inbox countdown and the thinking step as before.
  - Esc in the HUD after the user clicked it. The frontend's `wantsKey` includes `listening`, which only decides which keys the webview handles. Rust's `Hud::wants_key` stays false.
  - The HUD's Cancel button. With the inbox closed and the HUD not clicked, Esc belongs to the frontmost app; the hold key's release, a second press of the toggle, or the Cancel button stop the recording.
  `Controller::cancel(None)` also goes through the recorder when a recording is active.
- **S26. The event tap has a serialized lifecycle.** (New after the Sol review.)
  - Start and stop run one at a time on a lifecycle queue.
  - Each tap runs on its own thread with an immutable `TapContext`, so a reconfiguration never changes what a running callback reads.
  - Stop requests the thread's exit and waits for the thread to acknowledge it before anything is replaced.
  - A start that times out invalidates its token. A tap created after that is invalidated at once, never enabled, and its thread exits.
  - Before the app replaces the tap (settings saved, voice off), it calls `Controller::hold_lost`, so a held recording is cancelled rather than left waiting for a key-up the new tap never saw.

## Deviations pending user approval

These are product decisions, not technical limits. The plan builds them as stated; the user is asked before merge.

| Item | Spec | This plan |
|---|---|---|
| Minimum macOS (S16) | not stated; Tauri default 10.13 | 13.0 for the whole app |
| Toggle shortcut default (S12) | not stated | `Control+Alt+V` |
| Space as hold key in the open inbox (S13) | §8 default | deferred |
| Speech language, microphone device (S13) | §8 settings | English only; system default microphone |
| Missing English model (S21) | §9 "English until installed" | voice unavailable until the download finishes |

## Spec issues found

The spec cannot be built exactly as written in these places, or leaves them open. Each one is reported here; the spec itself is not edited.

1. **§4.1 lists Trigger, Capture and Transcriber as traits in `acd-voice`, with Capture producing "audio frames, level".** Trigger is an event type here (`TriggerEvent` plus the `Hold` state), not a trait: the sources (a Swift event tap, a Tauri global shortcut) push events into `Controller::hold` and `Controller::toggle`, and the rules are pure functions over those events. Capture and Transcriber are one Rust seam, `SpeechInput`, because with backend A the frames never leave Swift (S2); Swift keeps them as two protocols. §4.1 also asks backends to report their audio format. `Capabilities.sample_rate` reports only the analyzer's sample rate, for information; it does not describe the full format.
2. **§4.2 says "Rust holds opaque session handles".** The handle here is a `u64` generation, not a pointer (S3). It meets the same goal, and Rust never frees anything Swift owns.
3. **§6.2 does not say what happens to a recording cut at 60 s.** This plan discards it (S9).
4. **§6.2 does not define "silent".** This plan uses less than 150 ms of audio at or above -45 dBFS (S10).
5. **§4.4 covers a press during a countdown, but not during confirm, which-one or thinking.** S7 decides those cases.
6. **§8 sets Space as a hold key while the inbox panel is open, but gives no default toggle shortcut.** Space is deferred, and the toggle defaults to `Control+Alt+V` (S12, S13).
7. **§9 says for missing speech assets: "offer the system download; English until installed".** With English as the only language, a missing English model leaves nothing to fall back to. Voice is unavailable until the download finishes (S21).
8. **§6.4 says each permission "is requested when voice is first turned on".** For Input Monitoring, `CGRequestListenEventAccess` only opens a system prompt that points to System Settings, and the grant may need a relaunch (S17, spike item).
9. **§6.2 and §6.3 say locking stops capture and clears the HUD's last 5 utterances, but do not say what happens to the executor's own state.** Step 1 built no utterance history (D20). The executor still holds the frozen snapshot, the transcript, and any confirmation or countdown on screen. This plan clears all of them on lock and sleep, and cancels unsent voice countdowns and thinking steps (S24).

## File Structure

`crates/acd-voice` (cloud-ok, no platform code):

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `src/listen.rs` (new) | `TriggerEvent`, `Hold`, `toggle`, `Recorder`, `Stopped`, `Limits`, `judge`, `Heard`, `Progress`, `ListenEvent`, `ListenError`, `Capabilities`, `Unavailable`, `SpeechInput`, and `fake::FakeSpeechInput` (feature `testing`). |
| `src/lib.rs` | `pub mod listen;` |
| `src/hud.rs` | `Hud::Listening { id, partial, over }`. |
| `src/refusal.rs` | Listening refusals and `From<ListenError>`. |
| `src/plan.rs` | `Executor::listen`, `resume`, `listen_failed`, `is_idle`; `begin` reads the press flag. |
| `tests/executor.rs` | Press-time tests and the extended JSON contract. |

`crates/acd-voice-client` (cloud-ok):

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `src/controller.rs` | `attach_speech`, `hold`, `other_key`, `hold_lost`, `toggle`, `speech_event`, `capture`, `overflow`, `system_stop`, `escape_listening`, `notice`, `listening`, `Source`, `Admission`, `HudSink::listening`; `text_input` becomes a wrapper over `utterance`. |
| `src/lib.rs` | Re-exports `Source`. |
| `tests/common/mod.rs` | `Sink` records microphone changes and acknowledgements; `speech_rig`, `speech_rig_with`, `acked_until`, `final_`. |
| `tests/speech.rs` (new) | Controller tests with `FakeSpeechInput`. |
| `tests/logs.rs` | A spoken transcript never reaches the logs. |

Task order and dependencies:

- Task 1A, then 1B (the spike), run in parallel with Tasks 2 → 3 → 4 (Rust core, `cloud-ok`).
- Task 5A depends on 1B. Then 5B → 5C → 7A in order: all four edit the Swift package.
- Task 6 depends on Tasks 4 and 5C. Task 7B depends on 6 and 7A. Task 8 depends on Tasks 3, 4 and 6. Task 9 depends on Tasks 6, 7B and 8. Task 10 comes last.
- Tasks 6–9 all edit `app/src-tauri/src/voice/mod.rs` and `main.rs`. Run them one at a time, not in parallel.

---

### Task 1A: Bridge build and the spike probe

**Execution:** `local`. Builds and tests only; nothing is installed or run.

The probe lives inside the real app, because macOS ties privacy grants to the signed bundle that asks. A probe run from Terminal would attribute every prompt to Terminal. This task lays down the build plumbing (Swift package, `build.rs`, the C header) that Tasks 5A–5C and 7A extend, and the probe that Task 1B runs. `Bridge` here is a minimal version; Task 5B replaces its internals with the bounded outbox (S5) and keeps its API.

- Create: `app/src-tauri/swift/Package.swift`

- Create: `app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Bridge.swift`, `Permissions.swift`, `Locales.swift`, `Audio.swift`, `Probe.swift`

Create `app/src-tauri/src/voice/bridge.rs` with only its tests for now:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::mem::{offset_of, size_of};

    #[test]
    fn the_event_layout_matches_the_header() {
        assert_eq!(size_of::<VbEvent>(), 56);
        assert_eq!(offset_of!(VbEvent, kind), 8);
        assert_eq!(offset_of!(VbEvent, text), 24);
        assert_eq!(offset_of!(VbEvent, text_len), 32);
        assert_eq!(offset_of!(VbEvent, audio_ms), 40);
        assert_eq!(offset_of!(VbEvent, voiced_ms), 48);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_probe_report_is_copied_out_of_the_callback() {
        let json = br#"{"step":1,"microphone":"granted"}"#.to_vec();
        let ev = VbEvent { kind: VB_EV_PROBE, text: json.as_ptr(), text_len: json.len(), ..VbEvent::EMPTY };
        let decoded = unsafe { decode(&ev) };
        drop(json); // the borrowed bytes may go away once the callback returns
        assert_eq!(decoded, Some(BridgeEvent::Probe(r#"{"step":1,"microphone":"granted"}"#.into())));
    }

    #[test]
    fn null_text_and_unknown_kinds_are_safe() {
        assert_eq!(unsafe { decode(&VbEvent { kind: VB_EV_PROBE, ..VbEvent::EMPTY }) }, Some(BridgeEvent::Probe(String::new())));
        assert_eq!(unsafe { decode(&VbEvent { kind: 999, ..VbEvent::EMPTY }) }, None);
    }
}
```

```swift
// swift-tools-version: 6.0
// The voice bridge (voice spec §4.2, step-2 plan S1). Built into the app by
// build.rs through swift-rs; the C contract is VoiceBridgeC/voice_bridge.h.
import PackageDescription

// FoundationModels exists only on macOS 26. The probe (Task 1A) uses it, so
// it is weak-linked: autolinking is off, and the link names it with
// -weak_framework (plan S16). build.rs does the same for the app's link.
#if compiler(>=6.2)
let swiftSettings: [SwiftSetting] = [
    .swiftLanguageMode(.v5),
    .unsafeFlags(["-Xfrontend", "-disable-autolink-framework", "-Xfrontend", "FoundationModels"]),
]
let linkerSettings: [LinkerSetting] = [.unsafeFlags(["-Xlinker", "-weak_framework", "-Xlinker", "FoundationModels"])]
#else
let swiftSettings: [SwiftSetting] = [.swiftLanguageMode(.v5)]
let linkerSettings: [LinkerSetting] = []
#endif

let package = Package(
    name: "VoiceBridge",
    // The app's minimum macOS (plan S16). Voice itself needs macOS 26 and
    // checks at run time.
    platforms: [.macOS(.v13)],
    products: [
        .library(name: "VoiceBridge", type: .static, targets: ["VoiceBridge"]),
    ],
    targets: [
        .target(name: "VoiceBridgeC", path: "Sources/VoiceBridgeC"),
        .target(
            name: "VoiceBridge",
            dependencies: ["VoiceBridgeC"],
            path: "Sources/VoiceBridge",
            swiftSettings: swiftSettings,
            linkerSettings: linkerSettings
        ),
        .testTarget(
            name: "VoiceBridgeTests",
            dependencies: ["VoiceBridge", "VoiceBridgeC"],
            path: "Tests/VoiceBridgeTests",
            swiftSettings: [.swiftLanguageMode(.v5)]
        ),
    ]
)
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridgeC/include/voice_bridge.h`:

```c
#ifndef VOICE_BRIDGE_H
#define VOICE_BRIDGE_H
/*
 * The C contract between the app (Rust: app/src-tauri/src/voice/bridge.rs)
 * and the Swift voice bridge. Voice spec §4.2; step-2 plan S1 to S5.
 *
 * Ownership and lifetime:
 * - Swift owns every framework object, Task and AsyncSequence. Rust holds
 *   only generation numbers, never pointers to Swift objects (S3).
 * - vb_init is called once, on the main thread. `on_event` must stay valid for the life of the
 *   process. Swift calls it from one serial queue, never from the audio
 *   thread, and never re-entrantly. It must not block.
 * - vb_event.text is borrowed UTF-8, not NUL-terminated, valid only during
 *   the call. NULL when text_len is 0. The callback copies it.
 * - Every function returns at once. Results arrive as events.
 * - Nothing in an event is an error message: errors are codes (spec §6.3).
 */
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

/* Return codes, and vb_event.code for VB_EV_ERROR and VB_EV_ASSETS. */
#define VB_OK 0
#define VB_ERR_MIC_DENIED 1
#define VB_ERR_MIC_UNAVAILABLE 2
#define VB_ERR_SPEECH_DENIED 3
#define VB_ERR_SPEECH_UNAVAILABLE 4
#define VB_ERR_ASSETS_MISSING 5
#define VB_ERR_BUSY 6 /* reserved; no call returns it since S4 */
#define VB_ERR_FAILED 7
#define VB_ERR_INPUT_MONITORING_DENIED 8
#define VB_ERR_TAP_FAILED 9
#define VB_ERR_NOT_INITIALIZED 10

/* vb_event.kind, and what each kind sets. */
#define VB_EV_PARTIAL 1u      /* generation; text: the live transcript so far */
#define VB_EV_FINAL 2u        /* generation; text; audio_ms; voiced_ms; value: stop-to-final ms */
#define VB_EV_ERROR 3u        /* generation; code: VB_ERR_* */
#define VB_EV_CAPS 4u         /* code: VB_UA_*; value: streams partials (0/1); value2: sample rate; text: locale id */
#define VB_EV_PERMISSION 5u   /* code: VB_PERM_*; value: VB_PS_* */
#define VB_EV_HOLD 6u         /* value: 1 the hold key went down, 0 up */
#define VB_EV_OTHER_KEY 7u    /* another key went down; never says which (S11) */
#define VB_EV_TAP_DISABLED 8u /* code: 1 timeout, 2 user input; value: 1 if re-enabled */
#define VB_EV_SYSTEM 9u       /* code: VB_SYS_* */
#define VB_EV_ASSETS 10u      /* code: VB_OK or VB_ERR_* */
#define VB_EV_PROBE 11u       /* text: a JSON report with no transcript (Task 1A) */
#define VB_EV_CAPTURE 12u     /* generation; value: 1 the microphone started, 0 it stopped (S18) */
#define VB_EV_OVERFLOW 13u    /* events were dropped since the last delivery (S5) */

/* Permissions (vb_event.code for VB_EV_PERMISSION). */
#define VB_PERM_MICROPHONE 1u
#define VB_PERM_SPEECH 2u
#define VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING 3u

/* Permission states (vb_event.value for VB_EV_PERMISSION). */
#define VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED 0u
#define VB_PS_GRANTED 1u
#define VB_PS_DENIED 2u
#define VB_PS_RESTRICTED 3u

/* Why speech is unavailable (vb_event.code for VB_EV_CAPS); 0 = available. */
#define VB_UA_NONE 0u
#define VB_UA_OS_TOO_OLD 1u
#define VB_UA_SDK_TOO_OLD 2u
#define VB_UA_TRANSCRIBER_UNSUPPORTED 3u
#define VB_UA_LOCALE_UNSUPPORTED 4u
#define VB_UA_ASSETS_MISSING 5u

/* System events (vb_event.code for VB_EV_SYSTEM). */
#define VB_SYS_SCREEN_LOCKED 1u
#define VB_SYS_WILL_SLEEP 2u
#define VB_SYS_SCREENS_SLEEP 3u

typedef struct vb_event {
    uint64_t generation; /* the session's generation; 0 when not a session event */
    uint32_t kind;
    uint32_t code;
    uint32_t value;
    uint32_t value2;
    const uint8_t *text;
    size_t text_len;
    uint64_t audio_ms;
    uint64_t voiced_ms;
} vb_event;

typedef void (*vb_event_fn)(void *ctx, const vb_event *event);

/* Installs the callback and starts the bridge. VB_OK, or VB_ERR_FAILED if
 * on_event is NULL. A second call replaces nothing and returns VB_OK. */
int32_t vb_init(void *ctx, vb_event_fn on_event);

/* Task 1A spike probe; the report arrives as VB_EV_PROBE. */
void vb_probe_run(uint32_t step);

#endif
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Bridge.swift`:

```swift
import Foundation
import VoiceBridgeC

/// A Swift copy of a `vb_event`. `deliver` lends it to C for one call.
struct Event: Equatable {
    var generation: UInt64 = 0
    var kind: UInt32
    var code: UInt32 = 0
    var value: UInt32 = 0
    var value2: UInt32 = 0
    var text: String? = nil
    var audioMs: UInt64 = 0
    var voicedMs: UInt64 = 0
}

/// The one way out of Swift (plan S3, S5). Every event goes through
/// `events`, a serial queue, so the Rust callback never runs twice at once
/// and never on the audio thread.
final class Bridge: @unchecked Sendable {
    static let shared = Bridge()

    let events = DispatchQueue(label: "dev.agentcaller.voice.events")
    private let lock = NSLock()
    private var sink: ((Event) -> Void)?
    private var current: UInt64 = 0

    func install(_ sink: @escaping (Event) -> Void) {
        lock.lock()
        self.sink = sink
        lock.unlock()
    }

    /// The live session; session events of any other generation are
    /// dropped (spec §4.2). 0 means no session.
    func setCurrent(_ generation: UInt64) {
        lock.lock()
        current = generation
        lock.unlock()
    }

    var currentGeneration: UInt64 {
        lock.lock()
        defer { lock.unlock() }
        return current
    }

    /// An event of session `e.generation`: dropped unless it is the live one.
    func emitSession(_ e: Event) {
        events.async {
            guard e.generation != 0, e.generation == self.currentGeneration else { return }
            self.deliver(e)
        }
    }

    /// An event not tied to a session.
    func emit(_ e: Event) {
        events.async { self.deliver(e) }
    }

    private func deliver(_ e: Event) {
        lock.lock()
        let sink = self.sink
        lock.unlock()
        sink?(e)
    }
}

private let started = NSLock()
private var isStarted = false

@_cdecl("vb_init")
public func vb_init(_ ctx: UnsafeMutableRawPointer?, _ onEvent: vb_event_fn?) -> Int32 {
    guard let onEvent else { return VB_ERR_FAILED }
    started.lock()
    defer { started.unlock() }
    if isStarted { return VB_OK }
    isStarted = true
    let context = UInt(bitPattern: ctx)
    Bridge.shared.install { e in
        var ev = vb_event()
        ev.generation = e.generation
        ev.kind = e.kind
        ev.code = e.code
        ev.value = e.value
        ev.value2 = e.value2
        ev.audio_ms = e.audioMs
        ev.voiced_ms = e.voicedMs
        let ctx = UnsafeMutableRawPointer(bitPattern: context)
        guard var text = e.text, !text.isEmpty else {
            onEvent(ctx, &ev)
            return
        }
        // Borrowed for this call only (header contract).
        text.withUTF8 { bytes in
            ev.text = bytes.baseAddress
            ev.text_len = bytes.count
            onEvent(ctx, &ev)
        }
    }
    return VB_OK
}
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Permissions.swift`:

```swift
import AVFoundation
import CoreGraphics
import IOKit.hid
import Speech
import VoiceBridgeC

/// The three permissions voice may need (spec §6.4).
enum Permissions {
    static func status(_ permission: UInt32) -> UInt32 {
        switch permission {
        case VB_PERM_MICROPHONE:
            switch AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for: .audio) {
            case .authorized: return VB_PS_GRANTED
            case .denied: return VB_PS_DENIED
            case .restricted: return VB_PS_RESTRICTED
            default: return VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED
            }
        case VB_PERM_SPEECH:
            switch SFSpeechRecognizer.authorizationStatus() {
            case .authorized: return VB_PS_GRANTED
            case .denied: return VB_PS_DENIED
            case .restricted: return VB_PS_RESTRICTED
            default: return VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED
            }
        case VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING:
            // The only call that tells "never asked" from "denied" (plan S17).
            switch IOHIDCheckAccess(kIOHIDRequestTypeListenEvent) {
            case kIOHIDAccessTypeGranted: return VB_PS_GRANTED
            case kIOHIDAccessTypeDenied: return VB_PS_DENIED
            default: return VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED
            }
        default:
            return VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED
        }
    }

    /// Shows the system prompt if the user was never asked. The result
    /// arrives as VB_EV_PERMISSION.
    static func request(_ permission: UInt32) {
        let report = { Bridge.shared.emit(Event(kind: VB_EV_PERMISSION, code: permission, value: status(permission))) }
        switch permission {
        case VB_PERM_MICROPHONE:
            AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess(for: .audio) { _ in report() }
        case VB_PERM_SPEECH:
            // Needs NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription, or the process ends.
            SFSpeechRecognizer.requestAuthorization { _ in report() }
        case VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING:
            _ = CGRequestListenEventAccess()
            report()
        default:
            break
        }
    }
}

func permissionName(_ state: UInt32) -> String {
    switch state {
    case VB_PS_GRANTED: return "granted"
    case VB_PS_DENIED: return "denied"
    case VB_PS_RESTRICTED: return "restricted"
    default: return "not_determined"
    }
}
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Locales.swift`:

```swift
import Foundation
#if compiler(>=6.2)
import Speech

@available(macOS 26.0, *)
enum Locales {
    /// English only in step 2 (plan S13): the system locale if it is an
    /// English locale SpeechTranscriber supports, else en-US.
    static func resolve() async -> Locale? {
        let current = Locale.current
        if current.language.languageCode?.identifier == "en",
           let locale = await SpeechTranscriber.supportedLocale(equivalentTo: current) {
            return locale
        }
        return await SpeechTranscriber.supportedLocale(equivalentTo: Locale(identifier: "en_US"))
    }

    static func isInstalled(_ locale: Locale) async -> Bool {
        let installed = await SpeechTranscriber.installedLocales
        return installed.contains { $0.identifier(.bcp47) == locale.identifier(.bcp47) }
    }
}
#endif
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Audio.swift`:

```swift
import AVFoundation

enum Audio {
    /// A buffer counts as voiced at or above this RMS level (plan S10).
    static let voicedDBFS: Float = -45

    /// Converts one capture buffer to the analyzer's format; nil on failure.
    static func convert(_ buffer: AVAudioPCMBuffer, with converter: AVAudioConverter, to format: AVAudioFormat) -> AVAudioPCMBuffer? {
        let ratio = format.sampleRate / buffer.format.sampleRate
        let capacity = AVAudioFrameCount((Double(buffer.frameLength) * ratio).rounded(.up)) + 16
        guard let out = AVAudioPCMBuffer(pcmFormat: format, frameCapacity: capacity) else { return nil }
        var fed = false
        var error: NSError?
        let status = converter.convert(to: out, error: &error) { _, inputStatus in
            if fed {
                inputStatus.pointee = .noDataNow
                return nil
            }
            fed = true
            inputStatus.pointee = .haveData
            return buffer
        }
        return status == .error || error != nil ? nil : out
    }

    /// RMS level of the first channel in dBFS; -infinity for silence or a
    /// format without float samples.
    static func levelDBFS(_ buffer: AVAudioPCMBuffer) -> Float {
        guard let data = buffer.floatChannelData, buffer.frameLength > 0 else { return -.infinity }
        let n = Int(buffer.frameLength)
        var sum: Float = 0
        for i in 0..<n {
            let s = data[0][i]
            sum += s * s
        }
        return sum > 0 ? 10 * log10(sum / Float(n)) : -.infinity
    }
}
```

`app/src-tauri/swift/Sources/VoiceBridge/Probe.swift`. API names are from Apple's macOS 26 documentation and WWDC25 session 277. If the installed SDK names one differently, use the SDK's name and note the difference in the spike record.

```swift
import AVFoundation
import CoreGraphics
import Foundation
import Speech
import VoiceBridgeC
#if canImport(FoundationModels)
import FoundationModels
#endif

/// The step-2 spike (plan Task 1A). A report carries statuses, counts,
/// timings and a `matched` flag; never a transcript (spec §3.7). One probe
/// runs at a time (S20).
enum Probe {
    private static let lock = NSLock()
    private static var running = false
    /// Task 5B sets this to "a voice session exists", so a probe never
    /// opens the microphone next to one.
    static var sessionsLive: () -> Bool = { false }

    static func run(_ step: UInt32) {
        lock.lock()
        let busy = running
        if !busy { running = true }
        lock.unlock()
        if busy {
            emit(["error": "busy", "step": Int(step)])
            return
        }
        Task.detached {
            var report: [String: Any]
            switch step {
            case 1: report = await status()
            case 2: report = await ask(VB_PERM_MICROPHONE)
            case 3: report = sessionsLive() ? ["error": "busy"] : await live(seconds: 4)
            case 4: report = await clip()
            case 5: report = await ask(VB_PERM_SPEECH)
            case 6: report = await inputMonitoring(seconds: 10)
            case 7: report = foundationModels()
            default: report = ["error": "unknown_step"]
            }
            report["step"] = Int(step)
            lock.lock()
            running = false
            lock.unlock()
            emit(report)
        }
    }

    private static func emit(_ report: [String: Any]) {
        let data = try? JSONSerialization.data(withJSONObject: report, options: [.sortedKeys])
        let json = data.flatMap { String(data: $0, encoding: .utf8) } ?? "{}"
        Bridge.shared.emit(Event(kind: VB_EV_PROBE, text: json))
    }

    /// Spec §11 item 2: Foundation Models availability in the bundle.
    /// Reports the case name only.
    static func foundationModels() -> [String: Any] {
        #if canImport(FoundationModels)
        if #available(macOS 26.0, *) {
            switch SystemLanguageModel.default.availability {
            case .available:
                return ["foundation_models": "available"]
            case .unavailable(let reason):
                return ["foundation_models": "unavailable", "reason": String(describing: reason)]
            @unknown default:
                return ["foundation_models": "unknown"]
            }
        }
        return ["foundation_models": "os_too_old"]
        #else
        return ["foundation_models": "sdk_too_old"]
        #endif
    }

    static func ms(since start: ContinuousClock.Instant) -> Int {
        let d = start.duration(to: .now).components
        return Int(d.seconds) * 1000 + Int(d.attoseconds / 1_000_000_000_000_000)
    }

    static func permissions() -> [String: Any] {
        [
            "microphone": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_MICROPHONE)),
            "speech": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_SPEECH)),
            "input_monitoring": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING)),
        ]
    }

    static func status() async -> [String: Any] {
        var r = permissions()
        r["bundle"] = Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier ?? "none"
        r["os"] = ProcessInfo.processInfo.operatingSystemVersionString
        r["mic_usage_string"] = Bundle.main.object(forInfoDictionaryKey: "NSMicrophoneUsageDescription") != nil
        r["speech_usage_string"] = Bundle.main.object(forInfoDictionaryKey: "NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription") != nil
        #if compiler(>=6.2)
        if #available(macOS 26.0, *) {
            r["transcriber_available"] = SpeechTranscriber.isAvailable
            let english = { (l: Locale) in l.language.languageCode?.identifier == "en" }
            r["supported_count"] = await SpeechTranscriber.supportedLocales.count
            r["supported_en"] = await SpeechTranscriber.supportedLocales.filter(english).map { $0.identifier }.sorted()
            r["installed_en"] = await SpeechTranscriber.installedLocales.filter(english).map { $0.identifier }.sorted()
            r["resolved_locale"] = await Locales.resolve()?.identifier ?? "none"
        } else {
            r["reason"] = "os_too_old"
        }
        #else
        r["reason"] = "sdk_too_old"
        #endif
        return r
    }

    static func ask(_ permission: UInt32) async -> [String: Any] {
        let before = permissionName(Permissions.status(permission))
        await withCheckedContinuation { (done: CheckedContinuation<Void, Never>) in
            switch permission {
            case VB_PERM_MICROPHONE: AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess(for: .audio) { _ in done.resume() }
            case VB_PERM_SPEECH: SFSpeechRecognizer.requestAuthorization { _ in done.resume() }
            default: done.resume()
            }
        }
        return ["before": before, "after": permissionName(Permissions.status(permission))]
    }

    /// Speak "approve the request" right after starting. Measures partials
    /// and the time from stop to the final text. Speech Recognition is
    /// neither asked nor checked first: this run decides S14.
    static func live(seconds: Double) async -> [String: Any] {
        #if compiler(>=6.2)
        guard #available(macOS 26.0, *) else { return ["error": "os_too_old"] }
        var r = permissions()
        guard let locale = await Locales.resolve() else { return r.merging(["error": "no_locale"]) { $1 } }
        let transcriber = SpeechTranscriber(locale: locale, transcriptionOptions: [], reportingOptions: [.volatileResults], attributeOptions: [])
        let analyzer = SpeechAnalyzer(modules: [transcriber])
        guard let format = await SpeechAnalyzer.bestAvailableAudioFormat(compatibleWith: [transcriber]) else {
            return r.merging(["error": "no_format"]) { $1 }
        }
        let engine = AVAudioEngine()
        let input = engine.inputNode
        let micFormat = input.outputFormat(forBus: 0)
        guard micFormat.sampleRate > 0, let converter = AVAudioConverter(from: micFormat, to: format) else {
            return r.merging(["error": "no_microphone"]) { $1 }
        }
        let (inputs, feed) = AsyncStream.makeStream(of: AnalyzerInput.self, bufferingPolicy: .bufferingNewest(64))
        // The probe converts on the audio thread, as Task 5A's capture does.
        input.installTap(onBus: 0, bufferSize: 4096, format: micFormat) { buffer, _ in
            if let out = Audio.convert(buffer, with: converter, to: format) { feed.yield(AnalyzerInput(buffer: out)) }
        }
        let started = ContinuousClock.now
        let reader = Task { () -> (Int, Int, String, String) in
            var partials = 0
            var first = -1
            var text = ""
            do {
                for try await result in transcriber.results {
                    if result.isFinal {
                        text += String(result.text.characters)
                    } else {
                        partials += 1
                        if first < 0 { first = ms(since: started) }
                    }
                }
            } catch {
                return (partials, first, text, "results_failed")
            }
            return (partials, first, text, "none")
        }
        do {
            try await analyzer.start(inputSequence: inputs)
            try engine.start()
        } catch {
            input.removeTap(onBus: 0)
            return r.merging(["error": "start_failed"]) { $1 }
        }
        try? await Task.sleep(for: .seconds(seconds))
        let stopped = ContinuousClock.now
        engine.stop()
        input.removeTap(onBus: 0)
        feed.finish()
        do {
            try await analyzer.finalizeAndFinishThroughEndOfInput()
        } catch {
            return r.merging(["error": "finalize_failed"]) { $1 }
        }
        let (partials, first, text, error) = await reader.value
        r["locale"] = locale.identifier
        r["partials"] = partials
        r["first_partial_ms"] = first
        r["final_latency_ms"] = ms(since: stopped)
        r["final_chars"] = text.count
        r["matched"] = text.lowercased().contains("approve")
        r["error"] = error
        r["speech_after"] = permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_SPEECH))
        return r
        #else
        return ["error": "sdk_too_old"]
        #endif
    }

    /// A clip made with `say`: a repeatable baseline next to the live run.
    static func clip() async -> [String: Any] {
        #if compiler(>=6.2)
        guard #available(macOS 26.0, *) else { return ["error": "os_too_old"] }
        let url = FileManager.default.temporaryDirectory.appendingPathComponent("agent-caller-probe-\(UUID().uuidString).aiff")
        defer { try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url) }
        let say = Process()
        say.executableURL = URL(fileURLWithPath: "/usr/bin/say")
        say.arguments = ["-o", url.path, "approve the request"]
        do {
            try say.run()
            say.waitUntilExit()
        } catch {
            return ["error": "say_failed"]
        }
        guard let locale = await Locales.resolve() else { return ["error": "no_locale"] }
        let transcriber = SpeechTranscriber(locale: locale, transcriptionOptions: [], reportingOptions: [], attributeOptions: [])
        let started = ContinuousClock.now
        do {
            let file = try AVAudioFile(forReading: url)
            async let text = transcriber.results.reduce("") { $0 + String($1.text.characters) }
            let analyzer = SpeechAnalyzer(modules: [transcriber])
            if let last = try await analyzer.analyzeSequence(from: file) {
                try await analyzer.finalizeAndFinish(through: last)
            } else {
                await analyzer.cancelAndFinishNow()
            }
            let t = try await text
            return ["elapsed_ms": ms(since: started), "final_chars": t.count, "matched": t.lowercased().contains("approve"),
                    "speech_after": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_SPEECH))]
        } catch {
            return ["error": "transcription_failed", "elapsed_ms": ms(since: started),
                    "speech_after": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_SPEECH))]
        }
        #else
        return ["error": "sdk_too_old"]
        #endif
    }

    /// Asks for Input Monitoring, then counts Right Option presses for
    /// `seconds` with a listen-only flagsChanged tap. Hold Right Option
    /// three times. Only that key is counted; nothing else is recorded.
    static func inputMonitoring(seconds: Double) async -> [String: Any] {
        let before = permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING))
        let requested = CGRequestListenEventAccess()
        let counts = await FlagCounter().run(seconds: seconds)
        return ["before": before, "request_returned": requested, "tap_created": counts.created,
                "right_option_down": counts.down, "right_option_up": counts.up,
                "after": permissionName(Permissions.status(VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING))]
    }
}

/// A probe-only tap on its own thread and run loop.
final class FlagCounter: @unchecked Sendable {
    var down = 0
    var up = 0

    func run(seconds: Double) async -> (created: Bool, down: Int, up: Int) {
        await withCheckedContinuation { done in
            let thread = Thread {
                let mask = CGEventMask(1 << CGEventType.flagsChanged.rawValue)
                let me = Unmanaged.passUnretained(self).toOpaque()
                let callback: CGEventTapCallBack = { _, type, event, info in
                    if type == .flagsChanged, event.getIntegerValueField(.keyboardEventKeycode) == 61, let info {
                        let counter = Unmanaged<FlagCounter>.fromOpaque(info).takeUnretainedValue()
                        if event.flags.rawValue & 0x40 != 0 { counter.down += 1 } else { counter.up += 1 }
                    }
                    return Unmanaged.passUnretained(event)
                }
                guard let tap = CGEvent.tapCreate(tap: .cgSessionEventTap, place: .headInsertEventTap, options: .listenOnly,
                                                  eventsOfInterest: mask, callback: callback, userInfo: me) else {
                    done.resume(returning: (false, 0, 0))
                    return
                }
                let source = CFMachPortCreateRunLoopSource(nil, tap, 0)
                CFRunLoopAddSource(CFRunLoopGetCurrent(), source, .commonModes)
                CGEvent.tapEnable(tap: tap, enable: true)
                _ = CFRunLoopRunInMode(.defaultMode, seconds, false)
                CGEvent.tapEnable(tap: tap, enable: false)
                CFMachPortInvalidate(tap)
                done.resume(returning: (true, self.down, self.up))
            }
            thread.start()
        }
    }
}

@_cdecl("vb_probe_run")
public func vb_probe_run(_ step: UInt32) {
    Probe.run(step)
}
```

`app/src-tauri/build.rs`:

```rust
fn main() {
    if std::env::var("CARGO_CFG_TARGET_OS").as_deref() == Ok("macos") {
        // The voice bridge (plan S1). swift-rs runs `swift build` on the
        // package, adds the Swift runtime search paths and links the static
        // library. Swift autolinks AVFoundation, Speech, CoreGraphics and
        // IOKit through its imports.
        swift_rs::SwiftLinker::new("13.0").with_package("VoiceBridge", "swift").link();
        // The package turns autolinking off for FoundationModels (plan S16);
        // link it weakly, if this SDK has it, so the app launches before macOS 26.
        let sdk = std::process::Command::new("xcrun").args(["--sdk", "macosx", "--show-sdk-path"]).output();
        if let Ok(out) = sdk {
            let path = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_owned();
            if std::path::Path::new(&path).join("System/Library/Frameworks/FoundationModels.framework").exists() {
                println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg=-Wl,-weak_framework,FoundationModels");
            }
        }
        println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=swift/Sources");
        println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=swift/Package.swift");
    }
    tauri_build::build()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The Rust side of the probe**

Put above the tests in `app/src-tauri/src/voice/bridge.rs`:

```rust
//! The C contract with the Swift voice bridge
//! (`swift/Sources/VoiceBridgeC/include/voice_bridge.h`). Plan S1–S5: one
//! `'static` callback, a bounded queue, text copied before the callback
//! returns. Nothing here logs event text.
use std::ffi::c_void;
use std::sync::OnceLock;
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};

use tokio::sync::mpsc;

pub const VB_OK: i32 = 0;
pub const VB_ERR_MIC_DENIED: i32 = 1;
pub const VB_ERR_MIC_UNAVAILABLE: i32 = 2;
pub const VB_ERR_SPEECH_DENIED: i32 = 3;
pub const VB_ERR_SPEECH_UNAVAILABLE: i32 = 4;
pub const VB_ERR_ASSETS_MISSING: i32 = 5;
pub const VB_ERR_BUSY: i32 = 6;
pub const VB_ERR_FAILED: i32 = 7;
pub const VB_ERR_INPUT_MONITORING_DENIED: i32 = 8;
pub const VB_ERR_TAP_FAILED: i32 = 9;
pub const VB_ERR_NOT_INITIALIZED: i32 = 10;

pub const VB_EV_PARTIAL: u32 = 1;
pub const VB_EV_FINAL: u32 = 2;
pub const VB_EV_ERROR: u32 = 3;
pub const VB_EV_CAPS: u32 = 4;
pub const VB_EV_PERMISSION: u32 = 5;
pub const VB_EV_HOLD: u32 = 6;
pub const VB_EV_OTHER_KEY: u32 = 7;
pub const VB_EV_TAP_DISABLED: u32 = 8;
pub const VB_EV_SYSTEM: u32 = 9;
pub const VB_EV_ASSETS: u32 = 10;
pub const VB_EV_PROBE: u32 = 11;
pub const VB_EV_CAPTURE: u32 = 12;
pub const VB_EV_OVERFLOW: u32 = 13;

pub const VB_PERM_MICROPHONE: u32 = 1;
pub const VB_PERM_SPEECH: u32 = 2;
pub const VB_PERM_INPUT_MONITORING: u32 = 3;

pub const VB_PS_NOT_DETERMINED: u32 = 0;
pub const VB_PS_GRANTED: u32 = 1;
pub const VB_PS_DENIED: u32 = 2;
pub const VB_PS_RESTRICTED: u32 = 3;

pub const VB_UA_NONE: u32 = 0;
pub const VB_UA_OS_TOO_OLD: u32 = 1;
pub const VB_UA_SDK_TOO_OLD: u32 = 2;
pub const VB_UA_TRANSCRIBER_UNSUPPORTED: u32 = 3;
pub const VB_UA_LOCALE_UNSUPPORTED: u32 = 4;
pub const VB_UA_ASSETS_MISSING: u32 = 5;

pub const VB_SYS_SCREEN_LOCKED: u32 = 1;
pub const VB_SYS_WILL_SLEEP: u32 = 2;
pub const VB_SYS_SCREENS_SLEEP: u32 = 3;

/// Mirrors `struct vb_event`; the layout is pinned by tests on both sides.
#[repr(C)]
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct VbEvent {
    pub generation: u64,
    pub kind: u32,
    pub code: u32,
    pub value: u32,
    pub value2: u32,
    pub text: *const u8,
    pub text_len: usize,
    pub audio_ms: u64,
    pub voiced_ms: u64,
}

impl VbEvent {
    pub const EMPTY: Self = Self {
        generation: 0,
        kind: 0,
        code: 0,
        value: 0,
        value2: 0,
        text: std::ptr::null(),
        text_len: 0,
        audio_ms: 0,
        voiced_ms: 0,
    };
}

pub type VbEventFn = unsafe extern "C" fn(ctx: *mut c_void, event: *const VbEvent);

mod ffi {
    use super::{VbEventFn, c_void};

    unsafe extern "C" {
        pub fn vb_init(ctx: *mut c_void, on_event: VbEventFn) -> i32;
        pub fn vb_probe_run(step: u32);
    }
}

/// What the bridge reported, copied out of the callback. `Debug` shows no
/// voice text: transcripts are `VoiceText` (Task 6).
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum BridgeEvent {
    /// A spike report: statuses, counts and timings, no transcript.
    Probe(String),
}

/// The queue from the callback to the app (plan S5).
pub const QUEUE: usize = 64;
static TX: OnceLock<mpsc::Sender<BridgeEvent>> = OnceLock::new();
/// Events dropped because the queue was full.
pub static DROPPED: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);

/// Copies `ev` into an owned event; `None` for kinds the app ignores.
///
/// # Safety
/// `ev.text` is null, or points to `ev.text_len` readable bytes for the
/// duration of this call (header contract).
pub unsafe fn decode(ev: &VbEvent) -> Option<BridgeEvent> {
    let text = || {
        if ev.text.is_null() || ev.text_len == 0 {
            return String::new();
        }
        // SAFETY: the caller guarantees `text_len` readable bytes.
        let bytes = unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(ev.text, ev.text_len) };
        String::from_utf8_lossy(bytes).into_owned()
    };
    match ev.kind {
        VB_EV_PROBE => Some(BridgeEvent::Probe(text())),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Swift calls this from its event queue. It copies and never blocks.
unsafe extern "C" fn on_event(_ctx: *mut c_void, event: *const VbEvent) {
    // SAFETY: Swift passes a valid event for the duration of this call.
    let Some(ev) = (unsafe { event.as_ref() }) else {
        return;
    };
    // SAFETY: same contract for the text it points to.
    let Some(decoded) = (unsafe { decode(ev) }) else {
        return;
    };
    if let Some(tx) = TX.get()
        && tx.try_send(decoded).is_err()
    {
        DROPPED.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }
}

/// Starts the bridge once. `None` if it was started before or failed.
pub fn start() -> Option<mpsc::Receiver<BridgeEvent>> {
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel(QUEUE);
    TX.set(tx).ok()?;
    // SAFETY: `on_event` is a 'static function and the context is unused (S3).
    let code = unsafe { ffi::vb_init(std::ptr::null_mut(), on_event) };
    if code != VB_OK {
        tracing::error!(code, "the voice bridge did not start");
        return None;
    }
    Some(rx)
}

/// Runs spike probe `step` (Task 1A); the report arrives as `Probe`.
pub fn probe(step: u32) {
    // SAFETY: takes a plain integer; returns at once.
    unsafe { ffi::vb_probe_run(step) }
}
```

```rust
/// Handles one event from the Swift bridge, on the async runtime.
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
pub fn dispatch(app: &AppHandle, ev: bridge::BridgeEvent) {
    match ev {
        bridge::BridgeEvent::Probe(report) => {
            if let Err(error) = app.emit("voice://probe", report) {
                tracing::error!(%error, "could not emit the probe report");
            }
        }
    }
}
```

Append to `app/src-tauri/src/voice/commands.rs`:

```rust
/// Spike probe step `step` (plan Task 1A, S20). Settings window only, and
/// only while voice and the debug setting are both on.
#[tauri::command]
pub fn voice_probe(window: WebviewWindow, voice: State<'_, VoiceState>, step: u32) {
    let allowed = {
        let s = voice.settings.lock().unwrap();
        s.enabled && s.debug_input
    };
    if window.label() != "settings" || !allowed {
        tracing::debug!(label = window.label(), allowed, "ignored a probe request");
        return;
    }
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    super::bridge::probe(step);
    #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
    let _ = step;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Run the probe in order (user)**

Open Settings. Turn on "Voice control" and "Show the typed command field". Click the probe buttons in this order, and paste each report into the session:

1. **1. Status.** Expect every permission `not_determined`, `transcriber_available: true`, and the English locales.
2. **3. Transcribe 4 s** before granting anything. Expect `error` or `matched: false`, since the microphone is not granted. Record whether any prompt appeared.
3. **2. Ask for the microphone.** Click Allow. Record the prompt's text.
4. **3. Transcribe 4 s.** Click, then at once say "approve the request" (about 2 s). This run decides S14, because Speech Recognition is still `not_determined`. Record `matched`, `partials`, `first_partial_ms`, `final_latency_ms`, `speech_after`, and whether a Speech Recognition prompt appeared.
5. **4. Transcribe a spoken clip.** Record `elapsed_ms` and `matched`.
6. **5. Ask for Speech Recognition.** Click Allow. Then run step 3 again and compare its latency and `matched`.
7. **6. Input Monitoring.** Allow, or use the button in the system prompt to enable Agent Caller in System Settings. Then click 6 again without relaunching, and hold and release Right Option three times within 10 s. Record `tap_created` and the counts. If the tap is still not created, quit and reopen the app, run 6 again, and record that a relaunch was needed (S17).
8. In Terminal, turn on Terminal > Secure Keyboard Entry. Click 6, hold Right Option three times with Terminal frontmost, and record the counts. Turn Secure Keyboard Entry off again.
9. **7. Foundation Models availability.** Record `foundation_models` and any `reason`.
10. Click 1 twice quickly. The second report says `"error": "busy"` or comes after the first; never two probes at once (S20).

  - `pub const MAX_RECORDING: Duration` (60 s), `MIN_UTTERANCE` (300 ms), `MIN_VOICED` (150 ms), `FINAL_TIMEOUT` (5 s).
  - `pub struct Limits { pub max: Duration, pub final_timeout: Duration }`, with `Default` set to the constants.
  - `pub enum TriggerEvent { Start, Stop, Cancel }`.
  - `pub struct Hold` with `fn key(&mut self, down: bool) -> Option<TriggerEvent>`, `fn other_key(&mut self) -> Option<TriggerEvent>`, `fn reset(&mut self) -> Option<TriggerEvent>`.
  - `pub fn toggle(listening: bool) -> TriggerEvent`.
  - `pub enum ListenError { MicrophoneDenied, MicrophoneUnavailable, SpeechDenied, SpeechUnavailable, AssetsMissing, Failed }` (`Serialize`, snake_case).
  - `pub enum Unavailable { Checking, NotMac, OsTooOld, SdkTooOld, TranscriberUnsupported, LocaleUnsupported, AssetsMissing }` (`Serialize`, snake_case).
  - `pub struct Capabilities { pub unavailable: Option<Unavailable>, pub streams_partials: bool, pub sample_rate: u32 }`, with `Capabilities::CHECKING`.
  - `pub enum ListenEvent { Partial { generation: u64, text: VoiceText }, Final { generation: u64, text: VoiceText, audio: Duration, voiced: Duration }, Error { generation: u64, error: ListenError } }`.

  - `pub enum Ignored { TooShort, Silent, Empty }`, `pub enum Heard { Transcript(VoiceText), Ignored(Ignored), Failed(ListenError), TooLong, TimedOut }`, `pub enum Progress { Partial(VoiceText), Done(Heard) }`, `pub enum Start { Started, Replaced(u64), Busy }`.

The cap is checked by `tick`, `stop` and `event` (S9).

Create `crates/acd-voice/src/listen.rs` with only the tests:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const fn ms(n: u64) -> Duration {
        Duration::from_millis(n)
    }

    fn said(t: &str) -> VoiceText {
        VoiceText::new(t)
    }

    #[test]
    fn hold_down_starts_up_stops_and_repeats_do_nothing() {
        let mut h = Hold::default();
        assert_eq!(h.key(true), Some(TriggerEvent::Start));
        assert_eq!(h.key(true), None, "auto-repeat of a modifier");
        assert_eq!(h.key(false), Some(TriggerEvent::Stop));
        assert_eq!(h.key(false), None);
    }

    /// Review Focus 2: Right Option + e types "é"; that is not a recording.
    #[test]
    fn a_chord_cancels_and_the_release_does_nothing() {
        let mut h = Hold::default();
        assert_eq!(h.key(true), Some(TriggerEvent::Start));
        assert_eq!(h.other_key(), Some(TriggerEvent::Cancel));
        assert_eq!(h.other_key(), None, "one cancel per press");
        assert_eq!(h.key(false), None, "the key-up after a chord neither stops nor routes");
        assert_eq!(h.key(true), Some(TriggerEvent::Start), "the next press works");
        assert_eq!(Hold::default().other_key(), None, "typing without the hold key is nothing");
    }

    /// Review Focus 5: a disabled tap may have lost the key-up.
    #[test]
    fn hold_reset_while_held_cancels() {
        let mut h = Hold::default();
        h.key(true);
        assert_eq!(h.reset(), Some(TriggerEvent::Cancel));
        assert_eq!(h.key(false), None);
        assert_eq!(Hold::default().reset(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_toggle_starts_then_stops() {
        assert_eq!(toggle(false), TriggerEvent::Start);
        assert_eq!(toggle(true), TriggerEvent::Stop);
    }

    #[test]
    fn short_silent_and_empty_utterances_are_ignored() {
        assert_eq!(judge(said("approve"), ms(299), ms(299)), Heard::Ignored(Ignored::TooShort));
        assert_eq!(judge(said("approve"), ms(300), ms(149)), Heard::Ignored(Ignored::Silent));
        assert_eq!(judge(said("  \n"), ms(900), ms(600)), Heard::Ignored(Ignored::Empty));
        assert_eq!(judge(said("approve"), ms(300), ms(150)), Heard::Transcript(said("approve")));
    }

    /// Review Focus 1: a lost key-up never leaves the microphone on, and
    /// what was recorded is thrown away (S9).
    #[test]
    fn a_recording_past_60_s_is_cut_and_discarded() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        assert_eq!(r.start(1, t0), Start::Started);
        assert_eq!(r.tick(t0 + MAX_RECORDING - ms(1)), None);
        assert_eq!(r.tick(t0 + MAX_RECORDING), Some((1, Heard::TooLong)));
        assert_eq!(r.active(), None);
        let late = ListenEvent::Final { generation: 1, text: said("approve"), audio: ms(60_000), voiced: ms(30_000) };
        assert_eq!(r.event(late, t0 + MAX_RECORDING), None, "nothing routes after the cap");
    }

    /// Review Focus 4.
    #[test]
    fn events_from_an_older_session_are_dropped() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.cancel(), Some(1));
        r.start(2, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Partial { generation: 1, text: said("app") }, t0), None);
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Final { generation: 1, text: said("approve"), audio: ms(900), voiced: ms(600) }, t0), None);
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Error { generation: 1, error: ListenError::Failed }, t0), None);
        assert_eq!(r.listening(), Some(2), "an old event does not end the new recording");
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Partial { generation: 2, text: said("de") }, t0), Some(Progress::Partial(said("de"))));
        r.stop(t0 + ms(800));
        let done = r.event(ListenEvent::Final { generation: 2, text: said("deny"), audio: ms(800), voiced: ms(500) }, t0);
        assert_eq!(done, Some(Progress::Done(Heard::Transcript(said("deny")))));
        assert_eq!(r.active(), None);
    }

    /// Review Focus 1, plan S9: a release processed after 60 s, before the
    /// timer ran, is too long. The order of timer and release does not matter.
    #[test]
    fn a_release_after_the_cap_is_too_long_even_before_the_timer() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.stop(t0 + MAX_RECORDING), Some(Stopped::TooLong(1)));
        assert_eq!(r.active(), None, "nothing waits for a final");
        assert_eq!(r.tick(t0 + MAX_RECORDING + ms(1)), None, "the late timer finds nothing");
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(2, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.stop(t0 + MAX_RECORDING - ms(1)), Some(Stopped::Finishing(2)));
    }

    /// Plan S9: a final is too long if it arrives after the cap while still
    /// listening, or if it reports 60 s of audio or more.
    #[test]
    fn a_final_after_the_cap_is_too_long() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        let fin = |audio| ListenEvent::Final { generation: 1, text: said("approve"), audio, voiced: ms(600) };
        assert_eq!(r.event(fin(ms(900)), t0 + MAX_RECORDING), Some(Progress::Done(Heard::TooLong)));
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        r.stop(t0 + ms(500));
        assert_eq!(r.event(fin(MAX_RECORDING), t0 + ms(600)), Some(Progress::Done(Heard::TooLong)));
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Partial { generation: 1, text: said("a") }, t0 + MAX_RECORDING), Some(Progress::Done(Heard::TooLong)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_final_times_out_closed() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.stop(t0 + ms(500)), Some(Stopped::Finishing(1)));
        assert_eq!(r.listening(), None);
        assert_eq!(r.active(), Some(1), "finishing");
        assert_eq!(r.tick(t0 + ms(500) + FINAL_TIMEOUT - ms(1)), None);
        assert_eq!(r.tick(t0 + ms(500) + FINAL_TIMEOUT), Some((1, Heard::TimedOut)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn partials_count_only_while_listening() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        r.stop(t0 + ms(400));
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Partial { generation: 1, text: said("app") }, t0), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_press_while_finishing_replaces_it_and_a_press_while_listening_is_busy() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(1, t0);
        assert_eq!(r.start(2, t0), Start::Busy);
        r.stop(t0 + ms(400));
        assert_eq!(r.start(3, t0 + ms(450)), Start::Replaced(1));
        assert_eq!(r.event(ListenEvent::Final { generation: 1, text: said("approve"), audio: ms(400), voiced: ms(300) }, t0), None);
        assert_eq!(r.listening(), Some(3));
    }

    #[test]
    fn errors_end_the_recording() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut r = Recorder::default();
        r.start(4, t0);
        let e = r.event(ListenEvent::Error { generation: 4, error: ListenError::MicrophoneUnavailable }, t0);
        assert_eq!(e, Some(Progress::Done(Heard::Failed(ListenError::MicrophoneUnavailable))));
        assert_eq!(r.active(), None);
        assert_eq!(r.stop(t0), None);
        assert_eq!(r.cancel(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn debug_never_shows_what_was_said() {
        let e = ListenEvent::Final { generation: 1, text: said("SECRET"), audio: ms(1), voiced: ms(1) };
        let h = Heard::Transcript(said("SECRET"));
        assert!(!format!("{e:?} {h:?} {:?}", Progress::Partial(said("SECRET"))).contains("SECRET"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_fake_records_calls_and_can_refuse() {
        use fake::{Call, FakeSpeechInput};
        let f = FakeSpeechInput::default();
        f.start(1, MAX_RECORDING).unwrap();
        f.stop(1);
        f.cancel(2);
        assert_eq!(f.calls(), [Call::Start(1), Call::Stop(1), Call::Cancel(2)]);
        *f.refuse.lock().unwrap() = Some(ListenError::MicrophoneDenied);
        assert_eq!(f.start(3, MAX_RECORDING), Err(ListenError::MicrophoneDenied));
        assert_eq!(f.capabilities().unavailable, None);
    }
}
```
